{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011Cg1bkPPQVHryQH7y1TxnR","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Links.md ===\n# FEATURE: Links\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Implement the look-for-link-or-image procedure and the inline, full, collapsed and shortcut link forms with the destination and title grammar. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Emphasis.md, FEATURE-Link-Reference-Definitions.md |\n| Provides    | look_for_link_or_image procedure, link destination and title grammar |\n| Consumes    | delimiter stack, process_emphasis procedure, reference map, normalize_label |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nImplement the Links section of the spec following the Appendix \"look for link or image\" procedure. The `![` machinery is shared with images; image rendering belongs to the images story.\n\n## Procedure\n\n- On `]`, search the delimiter stack from the top for a `[` or `![` opener. If none is found, emit literal `]`. If the opener is inactive, remove it and emit literal `]`.\n- Otherwise parse ahead for, in order: an inline link `(`destination, title`)`; a full reference `[label]`; a collapsed reference `[]`; a shortcut reference. References resolve through `normalize_label` and the reference map. A full reference whose label is not defined falls back to the shortcut form of the link text only if it is not followed by a label.\n- On success, build the link node from the inlines after the opener, run `process_emphasis` with the opener as `stack_bottom`, and remove the opener. For a link (not an image), set every earlier `[` opener inactive, so links never nest.\n- On failure, remove the opener and emit literal `]`.\n\n## Grammar\n\n- A destination is `<...>` with no line ending and no unescaped `<` or `>`, or a nonempty run with no space or control characters and balanced unescaped parentheses (at least three levels). An empty destination is allowed.\n- A title is double-quoted, single-quoted or parenthesized, with backslash-escaped delimiters, and no blank line inside.\n- Whitespace (spaces, tabs, at most one line ending) is allowed around the destination and title; destination and title must be separated by whitespace.\n- A link label has at most 999 characters and no unescaped brackets. No whitespace is allowed between link text and a following label or `()`.\n- Brackets inside link text must be balanced or backslash-escaped. Code spans, autolinks and raw HTML bind tighter than brackets.\n- Destination and title go through the shared unescape and entity helpers. The href is percent-encoded for non-ASCII and unsafe characters with the shared helper (an existing `%XX` stays as is), and the href and title are HTML-escaped.\n\n## Reads\n\n- Inline text, the reference map.\n\n## Writes\n\n- Link nodes in the inline tree.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\nThe Links corpus section includes image examples, so it is gated in the images story. The checks here are bounded.\n\n=== AC links-inline-destination-and-title ===\nIntent: An inline link renders one a element carrying the supplied destination, title and text.\n\nimport subprocess\n\ntext = \"label\"\ndest = \"/uri\"\ntitle = \"the title\"\nsource = \"[\" + text + \"](\" + dest + ' \"' + title + '\")\\n'\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=source, capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 1\nassert ('href=\"' + dest + '\"') in result.stdout\nassert ('title=\"' + title + '\"') in result.stdout\nassert (\">\" + text + \"</a>\") in result.stdout\n=== END AC links-inline-destination-and-title ===\n\n=== AC links-reference-forms ===\nIntent: Full, collapsed and shortcut references each resolve against a definition and render one a element.\n\nimport subprocess\n\ndefinition = \"\\n\\n[foo]: /url\\n\"\nfor use in (\"[text][foo]\", \"[foo][]\", \"[foo]\"):\n    result = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=use + definition, capture_output=True, text=True)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\n    assert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 1\n=== END AC links-reference-forms ===\n\n=== AC links-reference-case-insensitive ===\nIntent: Label matching ignores case.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"[FOO]\\n\\n[foo]: /url\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 1\n=== END AC links-reference-case-insensitive ===\n\n=== AC links-undefined-reference-literal ===\nIntent: A shortcut reference with no definition stays literal text.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"[nothing defined]\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 0\n=== END AC links-undefined-reference-literal ===\n\n=== AC links-no-nested-links ===\nIntent: A link inside link text wins and the outer brackets stay literal, so only one a element appears.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"[foo [bar](/uri)](/uri)\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 1\n=== END AC links-no-nested-links ===\n\n=== AC links-space-before-parenthesis ===\nIntent: Whitespace between link text and the parenthesis prevents an inline link.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"[link] (/uri)\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 0\n=== END AC links-space-before-parenthesis ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Links must never contain other links at any level of nesting.\n- Matching is on normalized label strings, not parsed inlines.\n- Follow the Appendix procedure; do not backtrack across the whole document.\n- Do not skip, filter or reinterpret corpus examples.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Images.md ===\n# FEATURE: Images\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Render images with plain-text alt attributes and verify links, images and emphasis against the conformance corpus. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Links.md |\n| Provides    | image rendering |\n| Consumes    | look_for_link_or_image procedure |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nImplement the Images section of the spec on top of the shared link machinery, and gate the Emphasis, Links and Images corpus sections, which are all satisfiable once images exist.\n\n## Rules\n\n- An image description starts with `![` and follows the same bracket rules as link text, except it may contain links.\n- All four forms apply: inline, full reference, collapsed and shortcut.\n- Render `<img src=\"...\" alt=\"...\" />` with ` title=\"...\"` only when a title exists. The src is percent-encoded and HTML-escaped like a link href.\n- The alt value is the plain-text content of the description: emphasis, links and nested images are reduced to their text content (nested images contribute their own alt), code spans contribute their content, and the result is HTML-escaped.\n- `\\![foo]` is a literal `!` followed by a link; `!\\[foo]` is literal text.\n\n## Reads\n\n- The inline tree built by the links story, the reference map.\n\n## Writes\n\n- Image nodes in the inline tree.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\nThe Link reference definitions section is gated in the tabs story because its examples need headings and quotes.\n\n=== AC images-corpus-sections ===\nIntent: The Emphasis and strong emphasis, Links and Images conformance sections (roughly 240 cases) pass emphasis$|^Links$|^Images$\",\n    ],\n    capture_output=True,\n    text=True,\n    env={**os.environ},\n)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<img \") == 1\nassert ('src=\"' + src + '\"') in result.stdout\nassert ('alt=\"' + alt + '\"') in result.stdout\nassert ('title=\"' + title + '\"') in result.stdout\n=== END AC images-inline-image ===\n\n=== AC images-alt-is-plain-text ===\nIntent: Emphasis inside an image description does not result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<img \") == 1\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<em>\") == 0\n=== END AC images-alt-is-plain-text ===\n\n=== AC images-reference-forms ===\nIntent: Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Implement hard line breaks, soft line breaks and plain textual content in inline parsing. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Images.md |\n| Provides    | hard break and soft break inline nodes |\n| Consumes    | inline scanner loop |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nImplement the Hard line breaks, Soft line breaks and Textual content sections of the spec.\n\n## Rules\n\n- A line ending preceded by two or more spaces, or by a backslash, that is not at the end of the block is a hard break, rendered as `<br />` followed by a line ending. The spaces or backslash are consumed. Leading spaces on the following line are dropped.\n- Any other line ending inside a block is a soft break, rendered as a line ending. Spaces at the end of the line and at the start of the next line are removed.\n- A hard-break marker at the end of a paragraph or heading is not a break: trailing spaces are dropped and a trailing backslash stays literal.\n- Breaks work inside emphasis and link text. They are inert inside code spans (the line ending becomes a space) and inside raw HTML tags.\n- All other characters are plain text. Internal spaces are preserved verbatim.\n\n## Reads\n\n- Inline text of paragraphs and headings.\n\n## Writes\n\n- Break and text nodes in the inline tree.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\nThe Hard line breaks section has an ATX heading example, so it is gated in the tabs story. The checks here are bounded.\n\n=== AC breaks-corpus-sections ===\nIntent: The Soft line breaks and Textual content conformance sections (5 cases) pass in full.\nSuite: scoped\nProbe: |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Parse thematic breaks of three or more matching -, _ or * characters and render them as hr elements. |\n| Depends On  | spec as a registered block start rule.\n\n## Rules\n\n- A thematic break is a line with up to three spaces of indentation, then three or more matching `-`, `_` or `*` characters, each optionally followed by any number of spaces or tabs. No other characters may appear on the line.\n- All non-space characters must be the same. Four or more spaces of indentation is not a thematic break.\n- A thematic break can interrupt a paragraph and needs no blank lines around it.\n- When a line could be a thematic break or a list item, the thematic break wins. When a line of dashes could be a thematic break or a setext underline, the setext heading wins; that wiring is added by the setext story.\n- It renders as `<hr />`.\n\n## Reads\n\n- Lines handed to the block engine.\n\n## Writes\n\n- Thematic break nodes in the block tree.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\nThe corpus section needs lists, setext headings and emphasis, so it is gated in the lists story. BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Links.md ===\n# FEATURE: Links\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Implement the look-for-link-or-image procedure and the inline, full, collapsed and shortcut link forms with the destination and title grammar. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Emphasis.md, FEATURE-Link-Reference-Definitions.md |\n| Provides    | look_for_link_or_image procedure, link destination and title grammar |\n| Consumes    | delimiter stack, process_emphasis procedure, reference map, normalize_label |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nImplement the Links section of the spec following the Appendix \"look for link or image\" procedure. The `![` machinery is shared with images; image rendering belongs to the images story.\n\n## Procedure\n\n- On `]`, search the delimiter stack from the top for a `[` or `![` opener. If none is found, emit literal `]`. If the opener is inactive, remove it and emit literal `]`.\n- Otherwise parse ahead for, in order: an inline link `(`destination, title`)`; a full reference `[label]`; a collapsed reference `[]`; a shortcut reference. References resolve through `normalize_label` and the reference map. A full reference whose label is not defined falls back to the shortcut form of the link text only if it is not followed by a label.\n- On success, build the link node from the inlines after the opener, run `process_emphasis` with the opener as `stack_bottom`, and remove the opener. For a link (not an image), set every earlier `[` opener inactive, so links never nest.\n- On failure, remove the opener and emit literal `]`.\n\n## Grammar\n\n- A destination is `<...>` with no line ending and no unescaped `<` or `>`, or a nonempty run with no space or control characters and balanced unescaped parentheses (at least three levels). An empty destination is allowed.\n- A title is double-quoted, single-quoted or parenthesized, with backslash-escaped delimiters, and no blank line inside.\n- Whitespace (spaces, tabs, at most one line ending) is allowed around the destination and title; destination and title must be separated by whitespace.\n- A link label has at most 999 characters and no unescaped brackets. No whitespace is allowed between link text and a following label or `()`.\n- Brackets inside link text must be balanced or backslash-escaped. Code spans, autolinks and raw HTML bind tighter than brackets.\n- Destination and title go through the shared unescape and entity helpers. The href is percent-encoded for non-ASCII and unsafe characters with the shared helper (an existing `%XX` stays as is), and the href and title are HTML-escaped.\n\n## Reads\n\n- Inline text, the reference map.\n\n## Writes\n\n- Link nodes in the inline tree.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\nThe Links corpus section includes image examples, so it is gated in the images story. The checks here are bounded.\n\n=== AC links-inline-destination-and-title ===\nIntent: An inline link renders one a element carrying the supplied destination, title and text.\n\nimport subprocess\n\ntext = \"label\"\ndest = \"/uri\"\ntitle = \"the title\"\nsource = \"[\" + text + \"](\" + dest + ' \"' + title + '\")\\n'\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=source, capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 1\nassert ('href=\"' + dest + '\"') in result.stdout\nassert ('title=\"' + title + '\"') in result.stdout\nassert (\">\" + text + \"</a>\") in result.stdout\n=== END AC links-inline-destination-and-title ===\n\n=== AC links-reference-forms ===\nIntent: Full, collapsed and shortcut references each resolve against a definition and render one a element.\n\nimport subprocess\n\ndefinition = \"\\n\\n[foo]: /url\\n\"\nfor use in (\"[text][foo]\", \"[foo][]\", \"[foo]\"):\n    result = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=use + definition, capture_output=True, text=True)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\n    assert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 1\n=== END AC links-reference-forms ===\n\n=== AC links-reference-case-insensitive ===\nIntent: Label matching ignores case.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"[FOO]\\n\\n[foo]: /url\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 1\n=== END AC links-reference-case-insensitive ===\n\n=== AC links-undefined-reference-literal ===\nIntent: A shortcut reference with no definition stays literal text.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"[nothing defined]\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 0\n=== END AC links-undefined-reference-literal ===\n\n=== AC links-no-nested-links ===\nIntent: A link inside link text wins and the outer brackets stay literal, so only one a element appears.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"[foo [bar](/uri)](/uri)\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 1\n=== END AC links-no-nested-links ===\n\n=== AC links-space-before-parenthesis ===\nIntent: Whitespace between link text and the parenthesis prevents an inline link.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"[link] (/uri)\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<a \") == 0\n=== END AC links-space-before-parenthesis ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Links must never contain other links at any level of nesting.\n- Matching is on normalized label strings, not parsed inlines.\n- Follow the Appendix procedure; do not backtrack across the whole document.\n- Do not skip, filter or reinterpret corpus examples.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Images.md ===\n# FEATURE: Images\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Render images with \"sources/spec_tests.py\",\n        \"--program\",\n        os.getcwd() + \"/commonmark\",\n        \"--spec\",\n        \"sources/spec.txt\",\n        \"--pattern\",\n        \"^Emphasis and strong text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<img \") == 1\nassert ('src=\"' + src + '\"') in result.stdout\nassert ('alt=\"' + alt + '\"') in result.stdout\nassert ('title=\"' + title + '\"') in result.stdout\n=== END AC images-inline-image ===\n\n=== AC images-alt-is-plain-text ===\nIntent: Emphasis inside an image description does not result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<img \") == 1\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<em>\") == 0\n=== END AC images-alt-is-plain-text ===\n\n=== AC images-reference-forms ===\nIntent: plain text only, never markup.\n- Do not skip, filter or reinterpret corpus examples.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Line-Breaks-Text.md ===\n# FEATURE: Line Breaks and Text\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Implement hard line breaks, soft line breaks and plain textual content in inline parsing. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Images.md |\n| Provides    | hard break and soft break inline nodes |\n| Consumes    | inline scanner loop |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nImplement the Hard line breaks, Soft line breaks and Textual content sections of the spec.\n\n## Rules\n\n- A line ending preceded by two or more spaces, or by a backslash, that is not at the end of the block is a hard break, rendered as `<br />` followed by a line ending. The spaces or backslash are consumed. Leading spaces on the following line are dropped.\n- Any other line ending inside a block is a soft break, rendered as a line ending. Spaces at the end of the line and at the start of the next line are removed.\n- A hard-break marker at the end of a paragraph or heading is not a break: trailing spaces are dropped and a trailing backslash stays literal.\n- Breaks work inside emphasis and link text. They are inert inside code spans (the line ending becomes a space) and inside raw HTML tags.\n- All other characters are plain text. Internal spaces are preserved verbatim.\n\n## Reads\n\n- Inline text of paragraphs and headings.\n\n## Writes\n\n- Break and text nodes in the inline tree.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\nThe Hard line breaks section has an ATX heading example, so it is gated in the tabs story. The checks here are bounded.\n\n=== AC breaks-corpus-sections ===\nIntent: The Soft line breaks and Textual content conformance sections (5 cases) pass in full.\nSuite: scoped\nProbe: --dump-tests\nProbe Count: lines\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nresult = subprocess.run(\n    [\n        sys.executable,\n        \"sources/spec_tests.py\",\n        \"--program\",\n        os.getcwd() + \"/commonmark\",\n        \"--spec\",\n        \"sources/spec.txt\",\n        \"--pattern\",\n        \"^Soft line breaks$|^Textual content$\",\n    ],\n    capture_output=True,\n    text=True,\n    env={**os.environ},\n)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 0\n=== END AC breaks-corpus-sections ===\n\n=== AC breaks-hard-break-spaces ===\nIntent: Two or more trailing spaces before a line ending give one br element between the two lines.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nfirst = \"alpha\"\nsecond = \"beta\"\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=first + \"   \\n\" + second + \"\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<br />\") == 1\nassert first in result.stdout\nassert second in result.stdout\n=== END AC breaks-hard-break-spaces ===\n\n=== AC breaks-hard-break-backslash ===\nIntent: A backslash before a line ending gives one br element.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"alpha\\\\\\nbeta\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<br />\") == 1\n=== END AC breaks-hard-break-backslash ===\n\n=== AC breaks-soft-break ===\nIntent: A plain line ending is not a hard break and both lines stay in one paragraph.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"alpha\\nbeta\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<br />\") == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<p>\") == 1\n=== END AC breaks-soft-break ===\n\n=== AC breaks-none-at-block-end ===\nIntent: Trailing spaces or a trailing backslash at the end of a paragraph do not create a break.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nfor source in (\"alpha  \\n\", \"alpha\\\\\\n\"):\n    result = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=source, capture_output=True, text=True)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\n    assert result.stdout.count(\"<br />\") == 0\n=== END AC breaks-none-at-block-end ===\n\n=== AC breaks-inert-in-code-span ===\nIntent: Trailing spaces before a line ending inside a code span do not create a break.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"`code  \\nspan`\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<br />\") == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<code>\") == 1\n=== END AC breaks-inert-in-code-span ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A hard break must never occur at the end of a block.\n- Spaces in the middle of a text line must never be collapsed.\n- Do not skip, filter or reinterpret corpus examples.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Thematic-Breaks.md ===\n# FEATURE: Thematic Breaks\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Parse thematic breaks of three or more matching -, _ or * characters and render them as hr elements. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Line-Breaks-Text.md |\n| Provides    | thematic break block |\n| Consumes    | block-type registry |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nImplement the Thematic breaks section of the spec as a registered block start rule.\n\n## Rules\n\n- A thematic break is a line with up to three spaces of indentation, then three or more matching `-`, `_` or `*` characters, each optionally followed by any number of spaces or tabs. No other characters may appear on the line.\n- All non-space characters must be the same. Four or more spaces of indentation is not a thematic break.\n- A thematic break can interrupt a paragraph and needs no blank lines around it.\n- When a line could be a thematic break or a list item, the thematic break wins. When a line of dashes could be a thematic break or a setext underline, the setext heading wins; that wiring is added by the setext story.\n- It renders as `<hr />`.\n\n## Reads\n\n- Lines handed to the block engine.\n\n## Writes\n\n- Thematic break nodes in the block tree.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\nThe corpus section needs lists, setext headings and emphasis, so it is gated in the lists story. The checks here are bounded.\n\n=== AC thematic-three-markers ===\nIntent: Lines of three or more matching marker characters each render one hr element.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nlines = [\"***\", \"---\", \"___\", \"_____________\", \" - - -\", \" **  * ** * ** * **\"]\nsource = \"\\n\\n\".join(lines) + \"\\n\"\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=source, capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<hr />\") == len(lines)\n=== END AC thematic-three-markers ===\n\n=== AC thematic-not-a-break ===\nIntent: Wrong characters, too few characters, mixed characters and trailing text do not produce hr elements.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nsource = \"+++\\n\\n--\\n\\n_ _ _ _ a\\n\\na------\\n\\n *-*\\n\"\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=source, capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<hr />\") == 0\n=== END AC thematic-not-a-break ===\n\n=== AC thematic-interrupts-paragraph ===\nIntent: A thematic break directly after paragraph text splits it into two paragraphs around one hr.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"Foo\\n***\\nbar\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<hr />\") == 1\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<p>\") == 2\n=== END AC thematic-interrupts-paragraph ===\n\n=== AC thematic-trailing-whitespace ===\nIntent: Trailing spaces and tabs after the marker characters are allowed.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"- - - -    \\t\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<hr />\") == 1\n=== END AC thematic-trailing-whitespace ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Mixed marker characters on one line must never form a thematic break.\n- Four spaces of indentation must never form a thematic break.\n- Do not skip, filter or reinterpret corpus examples.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ATX-Headings.md ===\n# FEATURE: ATX Headings\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261014 V1 |\n| Description | Parse ATX headings of levels 1 to 6 with optional closing sequences and render them as h1 to h6 elements. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Thematic-Breaks.md |\n| Provides    | ATX heading block |\n| Consumes    | block-type registry, inline parser hook |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nImplement the ATX headings section of the spec as a registered block start rule whose content is parsed as inlines.\n\n## Rules\n\n- An opening sequence of 1 to 6 `#` characters, preceded by up to three spaces of indentation, must be followed by a space, a tab or the end of the line. Seven or more `#` is not a heading, and `#hashtag` is not a heading.\n- The content is stripped of leading and trailing spaces and tabs before inline parsing.\n- An optional closing sequence of `#` characters must be preceded by a space or tab and followed only by spaces or tabs. Backslash-escaped `#` characters are not part of the closing sequence. A `#` run followed by other text is content.\n- The heading level equals the number of opening `#` characters. A heading can be empty.\n- An ATX heading can interrupt a paragraph and needs no blank lines around it.\n- It renders as `<hN>content</hN>`.\n\n## Reads\n\n- Lines handed to the block engine.\n\n## Writes\n\n- Heading nodes in the block tree; inline content is parsed in phase 2.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\nThe corpus section uses indented code and thematic break interactions, so it is gated in the lists story. The checks here are bounded.\n\n=== AC atx-levels-one-to-six ===\nIntent: Each opening-sequence length from 1 to 6 renders exactly one heading of that level.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nfor level in range(1, 7):\n    source = \"#\" * level + \" foo\\n\"\n    result = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=source, capture_output=True, text=True)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\n    assert result.stdout.count(\"<h\" + str(level) + \">\") == 1\n    assert result.stdout.count(\"<h\") == 1\n=== END AC atx-levels-one-to-six ===\n\n=== AC atx-not-a-heading ===\nIntent: Seven hashes, a missing space, and an escaped hash do not produce headings.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nsource = \"####### foo\\n\\n#hashtag\\n\\n\\\\## foo\\n\"\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=source, capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<h\") == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<p>\") == 3\n=== END AC atx-not-a-heading ===\n\n=== AC atx-closing-sequence ===\nIntent: A closing hash sequence is removed from the heading content.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"## foo ##\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<h2>\") == 1\nassert result.stdout.count(\"#\") == 0\n=== END AC atx-closing-sequence ===\n\n=== AC atx-empty-heading ===\nIntent: Empty headings are allowed, one per line.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"#\\n\\n##\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<h1>\") == 1\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<h2>\") == 1\n=== END AC atx-empty-heading ===\n\n=== AC atx-interrupts-paragraph ===\nIntent: A heading splits a paragraph without blank lines, leaving two paragraphs around it.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"Foo bar\\n# baz\\nBar foo\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<h1>\") == 1\nassert result.stdout.count(\"<p>\") == 2\n=== END AC atx-interrupts-paragraph ===\n\n=== AC atx-indentation-limit ===\nIntent: Up to three spaces of indentation are allowed before the opening sequence, and four are not.\n\nimport subprocess\n\nallowed = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"   # foo\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nrefused = subprocess.run([\"./commonmark\"], input=\"    # foo\\n\", capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(allowed.stdout)\nprint(refused.stdout)\nassert allowed.returncode == 0\nassert refused.returncode == 0\nassert allowed.stdout.count(\"<h1>\") == 1\nassert refused.stdout.count(\"<h1>\") == 0\n=== END AC atx-indentation-limit ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A closing sequence must be preceded by whitespace; `foo#` keeps its hash.\n- Heading content must go through inline parsing, never be emitted raw.\n- Do not skip, filter or reinterpret corpus examples.\n=== END ARTIFACT
